## Chapter 72: one-dimensional literal regularity classification, 26 September 2026

The book-local theorem
`thm:frontier-polyhedral-one-dimensional-literal-classification` completes
P05.1 in dimension one for the literal coefficient category.  Literal
regularity holds exactly in degree zero, equivalently exactly when the
balanced polyhedral space is compact.  In positive degree, set
`phi=max(gamma,C)` and take a proper rational convex combination of `gamma`
and `phi`.  Edgewise integral-slope concave roofs produce a bounded literal
obstacle whose exact envelope is that combination.  A nonintegral slope on

## Chapter 72: all-dimensional noncompact literal nonregularity, 26 September 2026

The book-local theorem
`thm:frontier-polyhedral-noncompact-literal-nonregularity` extends the
negative half of P05.1 from graphs to every noncompact finite embedded
rational polyhedral space.  A rational truncation of the reference supplies
a compactly supported integral gap.  Finitely many ambient integral lattice
cones majorize a rational fraction of that gap and make vertex contact; their
minimum with the gap is a bounded literal obstacle.  Vertex convexity on
bounded cells and direct obstacle contact on unbounded cells identify the
exact envelope.  Its nonintegral slope on a primitive rational segment gives
the quantitative separation `L*dist(q,Z)/4` from every literal convex PA
approximant.

The proof uses neither balancing nor weights, purity, degree, or local
relation cones.  Literal complete-affine regularity therefore fails in every
positive dimension.  P05.1 remains active for the compact
higher-dimensional literal case, the higher-dimensional real-PA
classification, arbitrary nonseparable product obstacles, and source
coefficient-convention repair.  P05.2 remains active outside its
one-dimensional real-PA classification.  The inventory remains 42 active
atoms in 12 environments, tiers `(0,3,7,15,17)`.  No model-verifier result

## Chapter 3: supported vertical intersections and model architecture, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 3; Chapter 4
is next. The principal mathematical repair distinguishes the supported
Cartier-divisor class
`D . [X] in A_n(|D|)` from its pushforward
`c_1(O(D)) cap [X]` on the total space. The model projection formula now uses
Fulton's supported Cartier-divisor projection formula before applying the
remaining Chern class operators. In particular, for the principal special
fibre, the supported class can carry the nonzero relative degree even though

contradiction between the proof of birational invariance and conservation of
fibre degree.

## Chapter 5: skeleta, refinement, and canonical weights, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 5; Chapter 6
is next. The monomial-minorant proof now descends from the localization by a
finite \(\mathfrak q\)-adic filtration whose graded pieces are free on the
surviving monomials, removing the false claim that the quotient itself is a
free module. Connectedness of the dual complex is proved through Stein
factorization and connectedness of the special fibre. The retraction text no
longer says that every residue class is collapsed to one skeletal point: it
records exactly which monomial boundary data are retained.

The cross-model polyhedral statements have been restricted to their proved
scope. Arbitrary dominating models give compatible piecewise-affine
structures after common rational refinement, whereas literal subdivision of
old cells is asserted only for projective toroidal modifications. The same
distinction is now reflected in Chapter 21. The former projection claim for
arbitrary bent rational PL functions was removed, since such a function may
require a higher model.

The canonical-weight section now uses the model-independent
Mustaţă--Nicaise weight function, states piecewise affineness rather than
facewise affineness in the presence of horizontal zeroes, and separates the
Kontsevich--Soibelman minimum locus from an arbitrary model skeleton. Exact
source locators are recorded for the BFJ compatibility and inverse-limit
interfaces, Berkovich deformation retractions and local contractibility,
canonical weights, and the dlt minimal-model identification. In the Hesse
cubic example, adjunction and the Poincaré residue now prove that the weight
is constant on the circle, and the one-dimensionality of every
pluricanonical space closes the argument that the essential skeleton is
exactly that circle.

## Chapter 7: nets, measures, capacity convergence, and extended-real interfaces, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 7; Chapter 8
is next. Directed sets are now defined from the outset as nonempty preorders,
compact subsets carry the inclusion order appropriate to exhaustion, and the
main-text subnet convention agrees exactly with Appendix E's order-preserving
tail-cofinal convention. The upper-semicontinuous limsup formula now names the
neighborhood--tolerance refinement that attains its inner supremum, and the
psh regularization preview states the required local uniform upper bound.

The Riesz interface is explicitly real-valued. Its direct uniqueness proof
uses a common neighborhood obtained from separate outer-regular
approximations, and the proper-pushforward lemma asserts change of variables
for nonnegative Borel functions and integrable signed functions rather than
for every bounded function against a possibly infinite measure. Weak
convergence on compact spaces, vague convergence by compact-support testing,
and narrow convergence by bounded-continuous testing are now distinguished.
The nonsequential compactness example proves that a weak limit of Dirac masses
is multiplicative, hence has zero variance and is itself Dirac.

The capacity section equips the extended real line with an explicit metric
for its order topology and formulates convergence in capacity for maps to an
arbitrary metric space. This removes undefined differences at infinite
values and makes the quasicontinuity calculus precise. The
Duistermaat--Heckman section now treats its Borel representatives and narrow
limits at the correct measure-theoretic level, distinguishes the concave
closed tail from the right-continuous strict tail, and records both atoms and
the direction of the one-dimensional density monotonicity. The extended-real
ledger replaces an inapplicable topological monotone-convergence citation by
ordinary measure-theoretic monotone convergence, gives a complete case split
for the limsup-of-sums inequality, and separates pointwise from
usc-regularized zero-scaling limits. Twelve reusable definitions, examples,
and remarks received semantic labels.

## Chapter 10: polyhedral forms, tropical spaces, and calibrations, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 10; Chapter 11
is next. Forms on a polyhedral support are now defined intrinsically as germs
in a direct limit over ambient neighbourhoods. Their equality relation is
tested after restriction to a common neighbourhood, and the sheaf proof now
uses a locally finite ambient refinement and partition of unity instead of an
undeclared fixed neighbourhood. Operations preserve the cellwise boundary
condition, and the pullback statement is scoped to maps carrying source cells
into target cells.

Tropical spaces now have a fixed pure dimension, and the transition theorem
distinguishes signed densities from positive measures. An orientation is a
choice of primitive generator of the top exterior tangent lattice, while a
calibration is the resulting oriented multiplicity. The factorization into an
orientation and a signed cycle weight is explicitly noncanonical until one of
the two is fixed. Accordingly, the former global assertion of exactly two
pairs is corrected to exactly two pairs on each facet, with independent
choices across facets. The proof that a tropical circle cannot embed in a
line is now a direct maximum-vertex argument from balancing and no longer
depends forward on Chapter 11.

The subdivision and pushforward arguments have been consolidated into single
proofs that establish invariance and functoriality. Cycle addition uses a
locally finite common overlay, the Gysin statement treats negative expected
dimension as the zero cycle, and the Bergman-fan discussion now says precisely
that a nonrealizable matroid cannot arise as the tropicalization of the
corresponding linear space, without excluding unrelated realizations. Six
reusable items received semantic labels.

## Chapter 19: curves, toric varieties, and abelian varieties, 26 September 2026

The mathematical audit corrected three substantive scope defects. The former
example of a model of $\mathbf P^1$ with entire special fibre $2Y$ was
impossible in the presence of a rational section. It is now a conditional
normalization check for a proper curve possessing such a model: if

$2e=d$; an honest model line bundle forces $d$ even, while odd degree requires
rational coefficients in this one-component configuration. The Tate-curve
piecewise-affine interpolants are now claimed to decrease only along a nested
sequence $N_k=N_0 2^k$; arbitrary $N\to\infty$ retains uniform and weak
convergence but not monotonicity. Finally, the abelian transfer statement now
requires $L$ ample with its cubical metric, exactly as required by the Haar
measure theorem. The mass ledger was updated from the false degree-one
$\mathbf P^1$ row to the general identity $2e=d$.

## Chapter 20: model forms and semipositivity, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 20; Chapter 21
is next. The central correction separates three levels that the previous text
had conflated. Real theta-psh model functions form a convex set. Rational
theta-psh model functions are additionally stable under finite maxima. The
maximum of two real model functions need not itself be a model function: on a
blowup of the projective line, the maximum of zero with a translated segment
coordinate has an irrational breakpoint. In an ample generic class, however,
fixed-form rational approximation shows that such a maximum is a uniform limit
of rational theta-psh model functions. The model-envelope theorem now uses the
rational subfamily for upward directedness and proves, by a small downward
rational shift, that it has the same supremum as the full real family.

The primary-source audit confirms that BFJ16 Theorem 4.3 contains the stronger
fixed-regular-model exact sequence used by the book. Its Künnemann step has
been rewritten with the correct correspondence: after a finite extension, the
auxiliary curve product carries a projection whose pushforward already lands
on the original variety and original model. The finite degree and preliminary
positive multiple are absorbed by rational coefficients, so no separate
descent assertion is needed. The positive-representative proof now uses
semipositivity only to make the starting representative nef, and the vertical
correction example states the attainable size-two repair and the sharp
non-arbitrarily-small obstruction separately. Chapter 21's opening and
skeletal maximum argument were synchronized with the repaired rational/real
interface.

nonsequential. The toric discussion separates the Gauss retraction inequality
from facewise convexity, the BFJ/CLD comparison distinguishes a global class
from a local sheaf without asserting nonexistence, and the hypothesis ledger

refinement scope: full-dimensional subdivision cells inherit the old norm,
new lower-dimensional cells may not, and the resulting metrics are
bi-Lipschitz equivalent for each fixed finite refinement.

for a curve node. The dimensionally forced correction is now explained in
the main proof, the chapter notes, and Appendix L.

identity of thresholds. The imported-input count, theorem labels, and

## Chapter 23: envelopes, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 23; Chapter 24
is next. The chapter now keeps the raw all-psh envelope, the real-model BFJ
envelope, and the rational-model GJKM envelope distinct at every statement
where the competitor class matters. A direct source check of GJKM19,
Definition 2.8, Theorem 8.2, and Section 9 confirms that the
positive-characteristic continuity theorem concerns the rational-model
envelope first and that comparison with the all-psh envelope comes later with
additional SNC and cofinality hypotheses. The positive-characteristic
theorem, hypothesis atlas, dictionary, and notes have been synchronized with
that source order.

The reduction from a continuous obstacle and a real model form to the
integral zero-obstacle theorem has been made quantitatively exact. The
approximating obstacle is rational, the shifted rational class is required
only to retain an ample generic class, and the class approximation is chosen
within $\varepsilon^2$ before setting $t_\varepsilon=\varepsilon$. Thus the
bracketing class converges to the fixed relatively ample reference and the
convex curvature correction is justified without silently declaring the
shifted class relatively ample. The formal dependence on the reference class
now takes place on a fixed common higher model with a uniform ample
neighborhood. Fixed-model envelope comparisons are conditional on existence,
as required by their definition.

The topological interfaces were also repaired. Pointwise convergence of
retractions for a bounded obstacle is no longer promoted to uniform
convergence; neither second countability nor a countably cofinal system of SNC
models is inferred from the standing hypotheses; and the fixed-skeleton
selection theorem records that both its Lipschitz constant and its chosen
sequence depend on the model. The upper-semicontinuous-obstacle corollary now
applies the upper-envelope theorem directly from its actual hypotheses instead
of invoking an inapplicable lower-boundedness lemma. The closing comparison
table states all nonemptiness and boundedness assumptions and was redesigned

zero from later test-ideal replacements under extra hypotheses. All 1,582
legacy inline delimiters were normalized, minor prose defects were removed,

## Chapter 25: capacity and quasicontinuity, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 25; Chapter 26
is next. The central correction concerns the decreasing model nets used in
quasicontinuity. A continuous approximant minus an upper semicontinuous limit
is lower semicontinuous, and ordinary monotone convergence cannot be invoked
for an arbitrary net. The fundamental-estimate applications now use the full
weighted decreasing-net continuity of Chapter 24 throughout. This repairs the
bounded quasicontinuity proof, the diagonal sequential regularization, and
decreasing convergence in capacity while preserving the necessary distinction
between nets and sequences.

Quasicontinuity is now defined for maps to the extended real line with its
order topology, and convergence in capacity is metric-valued, using the
Chapter 7 metric for extended-real potentials. Truncations therefore converge
without assigning a value to an undefined difference at a polar point. The
universal sublevel bound already yields quasicontinuity of every
$\theta$-psh function, so the former forward dependency on Chapter 26 has been
removed; finite energy contributes a sharper tail estimate later, not the
validity of quasicontinuity. The scope discussion also records that
positive-characteristic regularization is only one of several inputs needed
to transport the full chapter.

The source audit checked arXiv:1201.0188 directly: BFJ15 Theorem 3.1 supplies
the weighted decreasing-net limit and Proposition 4.3 uses it in precisely the
fixed-entry form required here. Thuillier's Proposition 3.4.10 and Theorem
3.6.11 were rechecked for the curve polar-point and capacity-zero statements.
The all-psh extended-real quasicontinuity theorem is explicitly identified as
a book-local truncation corollary rather than a finite-energy import.

from full nonpluripolar Monge--Amp\`ere mass. The bounded theory records the
actual mixed-energy and intersection-theoretic inputs, while the extended
energy proof handles infinite endpoint values and product-directed decreasing
nets explicitly. Its monotone-continuity step is logically independent of the
concavity argument that it later completes.

nonpluripolar measure uses Borel strict superlevels without calling them open,
and the finite-energy capacity tail follows directly from the bounded cocycle
formula. Mixed finite-energy products now pass to the limit through a uniform
tail estimate before invoking weak convergence. The compactness corollary for
arbitrary nets normalizes by the varying suprema, extracts in the compact
product of the normalized superlevel and the bounded constants, and first
chooses a subnet realizing the original energy limsup. This supplies the
claimed upper-semicontinuity inequality for the original net rather than only
for an unspecified subnet.

The worked examples now exhibit both sides of the mass boundary. The polar-ray
example has infinite energy and loses nonpluripolar mass, whereas the new
slope-tower example has full mass but harmonic-series energy divergence. Thus
the book proves internally that the full-mass class is strictly larger than

separates Thuillier's finite-energy measures from finite-energy potentials,

## Chapter 27: differentiability, orthogonality, and the variational equation, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 27; Chapter 28
is next. The chapter now separates four functions that informal accounts often
merge: energy along an $\mathcal E^1$ segment, energy after projecting a
continuous obstacle, integration of that projection against a fixed measure,
and the variational functional.  The dependency atlas follows this distinction
through the two-sided energy sandwich, superdifferentials, orthogonality,
projected differentiability, finite-energy variations, and the later existence
theorem.  The singleton-supergradient corollary was moved after the
continuous-direction theorem: in its former position its proof silently used
the model-direction theorem at an arbitrary continuous base obstacle.

The limiting arguments now distinguish exactly where sequences and nets are
needed.  Model approximation in the continuous-direction theorem is
sequential, so dominated convergence would suffice there.  The decreasing
regularization of a finite-energy obstacle can be a genuinely
non-countably-cofinal net and still needs the monotone concave-net lemma.  The
remaining continuity in the real parameter is proved by the full subsequence
criterion: from every subsequence one extracts a further summable subsequence,
and the monotone-envelope construction gives the same target limit.  This
replaces the former inference from convergence along one selected subsequence.

The source audit checked BFJ15 Proposition 6.12, Theorem 7.2, Corollary 7.3,
and Appendix A directly.  It also repaired the later provenance: BGGJKM20
Theorem 5.4.3 is the non-Archimedean volume differentiability theorem,
Theorem 6.3.2 converts that input and continuity of the semipositive envelope
into metric orthogonality, and Theorem 6.3.3 gives the residue-characteristic-
zero BFJ formulation without algebraizability.  The GJKM19 positive-
characteristic result uses this later volume route after test-ideal envelope
continuity and the stated resolution hypotheses; it is not an extension of

## Chapter 28: finite-energy measures and variational solutions, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 28; Chapter 29
is next. The logical architecture now distinguishes finite dual energy,
admissibility, existence of a maximizer, solvability, and continuity of the
solution. In particular, the former claim that admissible and solvable
measures were incomparable has been corrected: under orthogonality every
admissible measure is solvable, while the unbounded slope-tower example is a
solvable measure whose uniform-integrability constant is infinite. The
rational-point example now proves exactly that a positive type-1 atom forces
infinite dual energy and explicitly declines the stronger unsupported claim
for measures that merely charge every neighbourhood of the point.

The one-dimensional arguments were tightened at both levels. Analytic
uniqueness for a connected smooth projective curve now follows directly from
$\ddc(\varphi-\psi)=0$ and Thuillier's maximum principle, without silently
assuming geometric connectedness, a semistable skeleton, or a cofinal
retraction system. The quantitative graph subsection states those additional
semistable hypotheses explicitly where they are genuinely needed. Its new
Green--Dirichlet lemma obtains edgewise absolute continuity, finite Dirichlet
energy, and the integration-by-parts identity for arbitrary positive Radon
data by rational atomic approximation. This closes the former gap in which
continuity alone was treated as control of the derivative integral.

The resulting estimates have their sharp constants:
$D_\Gamma(u_1-u_2)\leq L(\Gamma)\lVert\mu_1-\mu_2\rVert^2/4$ and
$\lVert u_1-u_2\rVert_\infty\leq
L(\Gamma)\lVert\mu_1-\mu_2\rVert/2$. The affine one-edge family attains the
second bound, and subdivision preserves both the total modulus length and the
coefficient. The dual-energy formula for arbitrary graph measures now uses
the same atomic approximation, energy Lipschitz continuity, and strong
derivative convergence. The completion theorem's source note was checked
directly against Boucksom--Jonsson: Theorems 2.23, 2.24, and 2.26 are the exact
components of its Theorem A. All 2,842 legacy inline delimiters were
normalized.

## Chapter 29: continuity of solutions, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 29; Chapter 30
is next. The chapter now distinguishes three logically separate notions:
continuity of envelopes, continuity of one prescribed Monge--Amp\`ere
solution, and continuity of the solution map. Envelope regularity is described
as an input to the supported existence-and-regularity argument rather than as
a bare implication to solution continuity; the chapter's own finite-energy
boundary example continues to show why that distinction matters.

The supported regularity proof now uses only the steps it needs. Decreasing
model regularization supplies the net, skeletal equicontinuity makes its limit
continuous on the fixed dual complex, Dini gives uniform convergence there,
and the domination lemma propagates the inequality to all of the analytic
space. The former global normalization and whole-space upper-semicontinuous
Dini step were redundant and have been removed. The uniform-model-sequence
corollary now chooses indices inductively so that they dominate their
predecessors as well as the index required at tolerance $1/k$. The abstract
iteration lemma likewise no longer assumes decay at infinity after its entry
point has already been supplied; the application records separately how its
capacity profile produces that entry point.

The hypothesis and provenance audit corrected two scope defects. In the BFJ
algebraizability signature, $K$ is the fraction field of
$\widehat{\mathcal O_{B,b}}$, not the completion of the local ring itself; a
proper global base is obtained only after compactifying the curve and the
spread-out data. The later results are counted consistently as the 2020
removal of algebraizability followed by three further routes: curves over
arbitrary complete nontrivially valued fields, equal characteristic $p$ under
geometric-origin and resolution hypotheses, and the conditional
mixed-characteristic theorem.

The curve theorem's explicitly unfinished proof reconstruction has been
replaced by a source boundary. The official published GJKM19 text was checked
directly: Theorems 1.1 and 3.1 give curve envelope continuity, Proposition 1.2
and Propositions 3.5 and 3.8 give the retraction mechanism used with
Thuillier's singular solution, and Proposition 3.7 gives the exact weighted
slope criterion retained in the chapter. Chapter 23's chord argument now
cites GJKM19 Proposition 3.10 directly, so it no longer depends on the deleted
local reconstruction. The source note states explicitly that descent,
value-group approximation, and comparison between Thuillier and model
semipositivity are imported rather than reproved.

## Chapter 30: functoriality and change of field, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 30; Chapter 31
is next. The opening and its comparison table now keep three operations apart:
a morphism of varieties over a fixed ground field, ordinary scalar extension
with its analytic projection, and the additional Gauss section for the
special extension $k\subset k((t))$ from a trivially valued field. Thus the
Gauss construction is a section of an ordinary base-change map but is not
itself induced by a morphism of schemes and is not available for a general
extension. The numerical ledger also records why finite and birational
projection formulae have different analytic scopes even though the latter has
degree one: a birational pullback of an ample class is generally only nef and
big.

The Gauss proof has been corrected at each analytic step. Semivaluations may
take real values or $+\infty$. Choosing a basis of the finite-dimensional
coefficient space gives a lower bound for every nonzero coefficient and hence
proves that the minimum in the Gauss formula is attained. The same finite
basis supplies a neighbourhood-uniform bound, so the infinite Laurent-series
maximum is the uniform limit of finite partial maxima. This replaces the false
global coefficient bound in the previous proof and establishes continuity at
the stated level. The centre argument now explicitly works on an affine
neighbourhood of the centre where the semivaluation is nonnegative.

The descent section now treats an arbitrary nontrivial singular psh potential,
not only a continuous one. Decreasing model regularization is averaged over
the finite Galois group, each invariant model potential descends under the
stated model-descent property, and decreasing stability gives the descended
singular potential. The surrounding questions distinguish uniqueness of a
descent from positivity and existence. The ramified discussion no longer
substitutes compatibility on a skeleton for descent of a model and its
Cartier data, and the infinite-extension discussion asserts only compatible
finite Galois stages rather than a quotient statement after completion.

The functoriality statements keep model, continuous, bounded, and
finite-energy products separate. The exceptional-mass warning is stated for
a model function and its model Monge--Amp\`ere measure, the level actually
constructed in the chapter. The official BJ22 publication record and its
section-level Gauss attribution were checked; the BFJ, BHJ, intersection-
theoretic, and base-change sources remain attached only to the scopes in which
they are used. The chapter expressly declines any identification of the full
trivially valued and nontrivially valued psh theories.

## Chapter 32: the trivially valued analytification, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 32; Chapter 33
is next. The chapter now distinguishes the valuation-theoretic structures that
are special to the trivial norm from features shared by general
analytifications. The affine-line classification correctly separates type III
and type IV points, and the curve section uses the general classification of
valuations on smooth projective curves. The proof that a finite surjective
morphism remains surjective after analytification now passes through the
nonempty completed-residue-field fibre. The discussion of the proper-support
locus no longer overstates the absence of Radon theory; it identifies the
missing compact global domain needed by the later compactness and energy
arguments.

The converse divisorial criterion now uses the Zariski--Abhyankar theorem,
whereas its inequality supplies only one direction. The deformation-to-the-
normal-cone discussion has been rebuilt on the normalized blowup: its
exceptional prime components give the Rees valuations, their multiplicities
fix the normalization, and the integral-closure formula takes the minimum over
all of them. A unique exceptional component is asserted only for a smooth
closed point. The quasimonomial cone is now obtained from closed orthants glued
along faces, with continuity at a face proved from the finite set of minimal
exponents furnished by Dickson's lemma. Coordinate independence is expressed
through the induced filtration by boundary ideals, and divisorial rays are
identified with primitive positive integral weights up to rational scaling.

The affine-plane table, midpoint calculation, and weighted-blowup discrepancy
now share that primitive normalization. The Gauss comparison distinguishes a
change in ground-field normalization from the scaling action and records the
exact relation $\sigma(tv)=t\sigma_{1/t}(v)$; its base-change warning has been
narrowed from a universal strict increase to the possibility of new
divisorial points. Redundant roadmaps, repeated summary ledgers, and evaluative
prose were removed, while the surviving dictionary gives exact internal proof
locations. The official BJ22 publication was checked directly in Sections 1
and 2 and Appendix A. All 2,871 legacy inline delimiters were
normalized.

## Chapter 33: test configurations, norms, and filtrations, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 33; Chapter 34
is next. The chapter now distinguishes the hypotheses carried by each part of
the test-configuration dictionary. Flatness makes the central fibre Cartier
and excludes vertical irreducible components, but does not exclude its embedded
points; that is supplied by the vertical $S_2$ condition. Equivariant weight
decomposition is stated on invariant affine charts. Integral closure in the
generic fibre is distinguished from normalization when $X$ is not normal, and
the morphism in the blowup reconstruction has the correct direction from the
dominating model to the blowup.

The filtration calculus has been corrected at its three main normalization
interfaces. A character twist translates weights, raw degree-$d$ base change
dilates them before integral closure, and a Veronese restriction carries the
old degree-$rm$ weights in new degree $m$. Accordingly the reindexed
Duistermaat--Heckman measure is the dilation by $r$, becoming unchanged only
after weights are divided by $r$ or normalized by the actual degree $rm$.
Degree-specific base ideals are retained until a generating Veronese is fixed;
after that choice their integral closures propagate with exponent $\ell$, not
$r\ell$. The central-fibre weight proof uses coherence and torsion-freeness
before applying Serre vanishing, and the finite-generation criterion now has
its continuity hypothesis and a complete support-set proof for the semigroup
algebra implication.

The Boucksom--Chen concave transform is treated as intrinsically upper
semicontinuous, and the probability, unnormalized, and Okounkov-body measure
normalizations are kept separate. The four worked examples now test the sign,
base-ideal, first-moment, and Veronese rules explicitly. In particular, the
product filtration uses an admissible strict upper linear bound, the
deformation-to-the-normal-cone example has
$\mathfrak a_{(2)}=\mathfrak a$ rather than $\mathfrak a^2$, and the toric
example first produces the tautological polarization for $4L$. The complex
geodesic discussion is deferred to Chapter 65 behind the existing erratum
firewall. The final dictionary marks only genuine equivalences as such and
records the Duistermaat--Heckman measure as a coarse invariant. Exact BHJ17,
BJ22, and BC11 locators were supplied, the official BHJ17 DOI and publication
URL were corrected, and all 1,840 legacy inline delimiters were normalized.

## Chapter 34: Fubini--Study, PL, and psh functions, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 34; Chapter 35
is next.  The chapter now distinguishes Fubini--Study, rational PL, real PL,
and psh functions before relating them.  The scaling endpoint at $t=0$ is the
trivial semivaluation of the closure of the centre of the original point; the
ray statement is made on $(0,+\infty)$ before that endpoint is attached.  The
proof also handles the case in which every surviving logarithmic slope is
$-\infty$.  The truncation example now records the correct decreasing limit.

Dual-complex faces are indexed by connected components of strata and the
retraction remembers that component.  Affineness on faces uses real-linearity
on real Cartier divisors, not a false denominator-clearing step.  The
arbitrary-characteristic comparison with test configurations is obtained by
equivariant graph closure, so no equivariant resolution theorem is assumed.
The closedness of the real PL-positive cone is taken from BJ22, Corollary 3.22,
instead of forcing an arbitrary convergent family onto one model.  BJ22,
Proposition 4.12 supplies decreasing-limit closure, the maximum principle, and
ray convexity with its scaling endpoint.

The central correction is fixed-class regularization.  BJ22, Theorem 4.15(iii)
gives, for every real ample class $\omega$, a decreasing net in
$\mathcal H_{\mathrm{dom}}(\omega)$ converging to any $\omega$-psh function;
it uses neither the envelope property nor unibranchness.  Chapters 45--48 were
revised accordingly.  Chapter 45 presents envelope regularization as an
obstacle-compatible alternative; Chapter 46 uses the unconditional net for
the extended pairing, upper semicontinuity, and homogeneity; Chapter 47 uses it
for the dual-energy test class; and Chapter 48 uses it for quasicontinuity while
reserving unibranchness and the envelope theorem for compactness, obstacle
operators, increasing envelopes, and the imported capacity implications.
Chapter 49 now points to the corrected generic-constancy label.

## Chapter 35: homogeneity, Legendre duality, and b-divisors, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 35; Chapter 36
is next.  The opening now explains one object through three distinct
mechanisms---the scaling action, Legendre slices, and numerical b-divisors---
without repeating an eight-item payoff list.  All 2,624 legacy inline math
spans were normalized, and the two formerly crowded dictionary tables were
replaced by description lists whose hypotheses remain visible.

The scaling and convex-analytic core was repaired in several places.  Weight
zero characterizes a homogeneous function even when a presentation contains
redundant nonzero weights.  Exact convergence along the scaling orbit uses the
trivial valuation and the endpoint is attached by the proved closure theorem.
In the finite elimination lemma, Fenchel--Moreau is applied to the displayed
polyhedral function rather than to its transform; coordinates equal to
$-\infty$ are handled by commuting the two infima, and continuity together
with $\max_i\lambda_i\geq 0$ justifies adjoining $t=0$ before projecting the
epigraph.  The divisorial comparison uses uniform PL density directly and
treats the identically $-\infty$ function separately.  Legendre reconstruction
first proves continuity of each finite orbit function and only then invokes
Fenchel--Moreau at the parameter value one.

The algebraic dictionary now matches its actual proof.  Finite attainment for
an ideal function is proved on the normalized blowup through the coherent
fractional ideal
$(\mathfrak a')^r\otimes\mathcal O_X(rcX_0-rD)$.  General Cartier b-divisors
are represented as differences of relatively semiample summands and their
coherent fractional ideals; the converse starts from the image of the global
evaluation map.  Numerical b-divisors carry modelwise finite support, the nef
limit proof separates dimension one, and monotone approximation uses
$C_i\downarrow B_\alpha-\overline H$ and
$B_i\downarrow B_\alpha+\overline D$, with convergence of base traces instead
of false termwise equality.  The homogeneous Siu proof is explicitly marked
as the stricter book-local statement, while the endpoint approximation is
imported from BJ22.

The forward and backward Legendre dictionaries now have the correct domain.
The raw transform $\Sigma(b,\lambda)=\sup_t(\phi_t+\lambda t)$ exists for any
family but need not be upper semicontinuous or psh; exact reconstruction
requires that raw transform to be psh, that its supremum equal the endpoint,
and the stated closure condition.  The envelope property guarantees only the
upper-semicontinuous regularization.  The general maximum principle is
attributed to the decreasing function and the minimal trivial valuation, not
to a homogeneous-only result.

## Chapter 37: envelope theorem statement and sharpness, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 37; Chapter 38
is next.  The chapter now distinguishes the geometric unibranch hypothesis,
the nonemptiness and common-bound conditions on the family, and the ambient
scope of Part IV.  Unibranchness is claimed sharp only where the published
ample-class converse applies.  The node--cusp example diagnoses the
normalization-reflection obstruction without being substituted for that
converse.  A nonempty psh family forces the class to be pseudoeffective but not
nef; the envelope-property statement is vacuous for a non-pseudoeffective
class.

The boundedness interface was corrected at theorem level.  The maximum
principle at the trivial valuation shows that pointwise boundedness of a family
inside one psh class is already a common uniform bound.  The projective-line
example therefore tests the actual failure caused by dropping the bound and is
now built from the Fubini--Study function already computed in Chapter 34.
Normalization of a compactness slice is kept separate from normalization of

## Chapter 38: Cartier b-divisors and homogeneous realization, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 38; Chapter 39
is next.  The chapter now presents the real Cartier-realization theorem as a
five-stage construction with an explicit dependency firewall: activate one
fixed pole, insert an ample buffer, choose ordered rational stages, take a
decreasing psh limit in one dominated class, and remove the pole by the global
homogeneous Siu identity.  The opening roadmap was shortened, the duplicate
roadmap section was removed, the final dictionary was converted from a cramped
three-column table to a term-by-term interface list, and all 2,220 legacy inline
math spans were normalized.

The mathematical audit repaired five substantive interfaces.  The activation
normalization now chooses an integer scaling factor, so the auxiliary divisor
remains rational.  Coefficient identities involving the fixed pole are formed
only on the full-support locus, where every term is finite; the analytic
identity on all of $X^{\mathrm{An}}$ appears only after realization as the Siu
sum and is never rearranged at a proper-support point.  Divisorial data are
said to determine a function only inside the psh class, not among arbitrary
upper-semicontinuous extensions.  The ample-buffer discussion now agrees with
the relative-to-absolute ampleness lemma: an ample base class plus the
relatively ample correction becomes ample for large scaling, and activation
normalizes the correction so that scaling one suffices.  Finally, the chapter
distinguishes bounded psh functions, which force nefness of the target class,
from the unbounded realization possible in a non-nef pseudoeffective class.

The published Boucksom--Jonsson source was checked directly at Theorem 4.22,
Corollary 4.23, Corollary 6.17, Theorem 6.21, Corollary 6.22, Theorem 6.32,
Lemma 6.34, Theorem 6.35 and Theorem 6.40.  The source note now separates the
Cartier-realization argument supplied by the envelope manuscript from the
published coefficient, comparison, regularization, and Siu-division inputs;
it also records that compactness in Corollary 6.22 carries the envelope
hypothesis.  The finished chapter was read continuously twice.  This is a
direct manuscript revision and primary-source audit; no independent-agent or

## Chapter 39: birational reflection and envelope descent, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 39; Chapter 40
is next.  The chapter now keeps three logically different statements apart:
homogeneous reflection under a projective birational morphism, reflection of
a psh carrier already belonging to the same numerical class, and descent of
the envelope property.  The revised dependency diagram records both uses of
the envelope property: it supplies a canonical psh carrier and it closes an
increasing bounded net.  The psh-space bijection alone is therefore not
presented as a proof of the envelope property downstairs.

The mathematical audit repaired six interfaces.  A fixed-class psh space is
an ordered convex set, not a cone; arbitrary positive combinations live in
the correspondingly graded class.  Monotonicity in the numerical class is
proved with the oriented perturbation $\eta+\varepsilon A\to\eta$ and rational
density in the ample cone.  The Fubini--Study comparison requires only that at
each point some generating section be nonzero.  The polar-locus statement is
restricted to the linear locus and does not promote a finite-stage equality
to a limit without proof.  The chapter dictionary no longer identifies every
homogeneous psh function with a single Cartier b-divisor.  Finally, the blowup
example derives coefficientwise convergence on its determining model and
hence the full divisorial trace on every higher model before applying
divisorial determination.

## Chapter 40: smooth varieties in arbitrary characteristic, 26 September 2026

The sequential chapter-quality programme has completed Chapter 40; Chapter 41
is next.  The duplicate roadmap subsection has been removed, leaving the
opening six-task map as the chapter's single structural guide.  The chapter
now keeps the common output of the multiplier-ideal and test-ideal engines
separate from the different mechanisms that produce it, and it states
explicitly that both the rational approximants and the finite-stage classes
dominate the target class in nef order.  Their distinction lies in the
limiting argument, not in opposite orientations.

The mathematical audit repaired five substantive interfaces.  The cusp
example now uses the exact $F$-pure-threshold formula from
Musta\c{t}\u{a}--Takagi--Watanabe and records that the exponent $4/5$ forces a
difference only in characteristic $5$: for $p>5$ with $p\equiv2\pmod3$, the
threshold $5/6-1/(6p)$ is greater than $4/5$.  Reduction modulo $p$ is
therefore rejected for the correct fixed-datum and uniform-in-$m$ reasons,
without the former false density claim.  The singular-scheme warning now
acknowledges broader multiplier- and test-ideal theories while isolating the
boundary-free four-property package needed here.  The worked cusp conclusion
compares the resulting ideal functions at the exceptional divisorial
valuation rather than invoking a vague Gauss extension.  Finally, the proof
concludes that the raw supremum equals the upper-semicontinuous limit, and
hence that its regularization is the same function.

weight is now described as the dimension of the local Artinian fibre factor
over the completed residue field, or equivalently as the sum of geometric
Artin lengths after algebraic closure.  This formulation retains
inseparability and valuation defect rather than silently replacing every
completed field degree by ramification index times residue degree.

étale locus and warns that an analytic fibre away from the branch divisor may
be one nonsplit point of weight two.  Its global identity is instead derived
from the determinant norm on the function field and the explicit norm-line
identification.  Finally, the projection formula requires only generic degree
for $q_*q^*=d$, whereas the divisor formula for a norm section uses finite
local freeness.

## Chapter 45: obstacle-domain and scope checkpoint, 30 September 2026

Chapter 45 remains **in progress**. This pass read the chapter continuously
and repaired four mathematical-domain or scope points. The increasing-obstacle
theorem now takes the supremum only over the cofinal tail $j\ge j_0$ on which
competitors are known to exist; the cited raw-envelope lemma applies to that
tail. The arbitrary-obstacle trichotomy identifies its $P_\theta$ as the raw
supremum, rather than silently extending the admissible operator. The
raw-supremum curve example explicitly assigns $-\infty$ at the closed-ray
endpoint. The class-perturbation warning now says that ampleness for
arbitrarily small perturbations forces nefness, instead of incorrectly
excluding ampleness at every fixed perturbation size. The failure-boundary
paragraph now attributes analytification's compactness to properness, not
projectivity, citing the book's properness theorem; the regularity example
specifies that its $C^1$-but-not-$C^2$ assertion is along the chosen ray.

The Chapter 40 rational-approximation proof was checked as a dependency.
Its difference $c_1(L)-\theta$ may be irrational; the class subsequently
multiplied to an ample line bundle is rational because it equals
$c_1(L)-c_1(E_i)$ for a rational line bundle and an effective
$\mathbf Q$-Cartier divisor. The proof now says both things explicitly.

## Appendices G--I: complete quality gate, 2 October 2026

Appendices A--I now pass the sequential programme. The pass repairs
tropical cancellation and calibration scope, exact complex relative-energy
and singularity interfaces, and the compiled-source Stacks dependency map.
All three complete sources were read: 13 assertion/proof pairs, 51 reusable
environments, 61 headings and 138 labels. Seven primary PDFs and 41 pages
were read and viewed in fourteen sheets. The 21 principal and 57
supplemental Stacks statements were checked; 0DSV is recorded as a rejected
citation, and two numerical false matches are excluded. Twelve finite
calculation families pass 118,721 example checks. The other 119 appendix
files, including all 108 frozen input fragments, are preserved.

The final full build has 4,164 pages and 19,445,205 bytes. All 46 appendix
pages and two seams were visually inspected in twelve sheets. Post-render
references pass for 425 inputs and 12,488 labels. Six checker tests, the
unchanged 42-atom inventory, scoped review and whitespace checks pass. No
scoped overflow remains. Eighteen inherited overfull boxes elsewhere and
the class-path warning remain for the whole-book pass.
